Once I start on those evenings, it... it really just feels like I’m on autopilot. I'll be sitting there, telling myself I need to stop because I have an early start with the crew the next morning, but I just keep going back to the kitchen for more anyway. It's like I can't stop myself once I've started.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about it in those terms, to be honest. I don't know if I'd say I feel guilty or ashamed... I mostly just notice how physically uncomfortable I am from eating so much. I mean, I haven't noticed a connection to my feelings or anything like that. It's not like I'm sitting there feeling sad or stressed out from the job site and deciding to eat to feel better. It's been bothering me, so I figured it was time to talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat this way when you're stressed or upset, or is it more about being tired from a long day?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really tracked it to see which one it is. I mean, managing a construction crew is pretty demanding, and the days are long, so I'm usually both stressed and tired by the time I get home. \n\nBut I couldn't say for sure if one specifically sets it off more than the other. It just sort of happens once I finally walk through the door and have a minute to myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating during these episodes, or does it feel like you can't stop once you've started?\n\nPatient: I definitely don't feel like I'm in control. Once I start on those evenings, it... it really just feels like I’m on autopilot. I'll be sitting there, telling myself I need to stop because I have an early start with the crew the next morning, but I just keep going back to the kitchen for more anyway. It's like I can't stop myself once I've started.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about it in those terms, to be honest. I don't know if I'd say I feel guilty or ashamed... I mostly just notice how physically uncomfortable I am from eating so much. I haven't really paid attention to whether there's a specific emotion attached to it after I finally stop.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in sex recently?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any changes there. Things have pretty much been the same as usual in that department.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating this way as a way to cope with feelings or emotions?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I haven't noticed a connection to my feelings or anything like that. It's not like I'm sitting there feeling sad or stressed out from the job site and deciding to eat to feel better. I mean, sometimes I am, just because I happen to be home by myself, but... I don't think I specifically make sure I'm alone or anything. It just happens whenever, really. I still keep going back to the kitchen for more. I just can't seem to stop once I get going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating very quickly during these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. After some really long days on the construction site, or sometimes after a neighborhood meeting in the evening, I’ll come home and just eat way more than I ever meant to. \n\nI'll just go through whatever’s there, really—leftover takeout, cereal, chips—and I don't stop until I’m uncomfortably full. It's been bothering me, so I figured it was time to talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat this way when you're stressed or upset, or is it more about being tired from a long day?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really tracked it to see which one it is. I mean, managing a construction crew is pretty demanding, and the days are long, so I'm usually both stressed and tired by the time I get home. \n\nBut I couldn't say for sure if one specifically sets it off more than the other. It just sort of happens once I finally walk through the door and have a minute to myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating during these episodes, or does it feel like you can't stop once you've started?\n\nPatient: I definitely don't feel like I'm in control. Once I start on those evenings, it... it really just feels like I’m on autopilot. I'll be sitting there, telling myself I need to stop because I have an early start with the crew the next morning, but I just keep going back to the kitchen for more anyway. It's like I can't stop myself once I've started.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about it in those terms, to be honest. I don't know if I'd say I feel guilty or ashamed... I mostly just notice how physically uncomfortable I am from eating so much. I haven't really paid attention to whether there's a specific emotion attached to it after I finally stop.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in sex recently?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any changes there. Things have pretty much been the same as usual in that department.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating this way as a way to cope with feelings or emotions?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I haven't noticed a connection to my feelings or anything like that. It's not like I'm sitting there feeling sad or stressed out from the job site and deciding to eat to feel better. It just... happens, I guess. I haven't really tied it to any specific emotions.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat alone when these episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, sometimes I am, just because I happen to be home by myself, but... I don't think I specifically make sure I'm alone or anything. It just happens whenever, really. I haven't noticed any kind of pattern with who's around.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating an excessive amount of food in a short period of time during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. That's actually what made me bring it up. Like, after some really long days on the site or after a neighborhood meeting, I’ll come home and just eat way more than I ever meant to. I'll go through leftover takeout, cereal, chips... just whatever’s there, really. And I'll just keep eating until I’m uncomfortably full.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if you lose interest in other things you normally enjoy on the days when these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I still go to work, still do my job... I still like doing the things I usually do. The eating thing doesn't really stop me from doing anything else or make me lose interest in my normal routine. It's just something that happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to resist the urge to continue eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's exactly what it feels like. Once I start on those evenings, it feels like I’m on autopilot. I'll be telling myself to stop because I have an early crew start the next morning, but I just... I still keep going back to the kitchen for more. I just can't seem to stop once I get going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating very quickly during these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm not really paying attention to the clock or anything, but I wouldn't say I'm eating unusually fast. It's more about the amount of food and just not being able to stop, rather than how quickly I'm eating it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the eating happens because you're not paying attention to your feelings after a long day, or is it more that you just can't control how much you eat once you start?\n\nPatient: Like I said a minute ago, it's definitely the control part. I don't really think it's about my feelings or ignoring them after work. It's just... once I start eating on those evenings, that autopilot feeling takes over. I can be standing right there literally telling myself to knock it off because I have to be up early for the crew, but I still end up going back to the kitchen for more anyway. I just lose the ability to control how much I'm having once I get going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating a lot?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel great about it because it messes with my sleep and I feel tired the next day at work, but I wouldn't call it guilt or shame. It's just frustrating that I can't seem to stop when I want to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any lack of interest in sex during the times when you're having these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any changes there. Um, my interest in sex is pretty normal. It hasn't really been affected by the eating stuff at all, as far as I can tell.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a lot happen?\n\nPatient: If I look back over the last few months, I'd say it averages out to about once a week. It fluctuates, though. Like, some weeks the whole pattern—the overeating and then purging—might happen twice, and then other weeks it won't happen at all. But yeah, on average, it's about a weekly thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or try to get rid of the food after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I just kind of panic about undoing all the work I've put into looking put-together, you know? I haven't noticed any kind of pattern with who's around.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating an excessive amount of food in a short period of time during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. That's actually what made me bring it up. Like, after some really long days on the site or after a neighborhood meeting, I’ll come home and just eat way more than I ever meant to. I'll go through leftover takeout, cereal, chips... just whatever’s there, really. And I'll just keep eating until I’m uncomfortably full.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if you lose interest in other things you normally enjoy on the days when these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I still go to work, still do my job... I still like doing the things I usually do. The eating thing doesn't really stop me from doing anything else or make me lose interest in my normal routine. It's just something that happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to resist the urge to continue eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's exactly what it feels like. Once I start on those evenings, it feels like I’m on autopilot. I'll be telling myself to stop because I have an early crew start the next morning, but I just... I still keep going back to the kitchen for more. I just can't seem to stop once I get going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating very quickly during these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm not really paying attention to the clock or anything, but I wouldn't say I'm eating unusually fast. It's more about the amount of food and just not being able to stop, rather than how quickly I'm eating it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the eating happens because you're not paying attention to your feelings after a long day, or is it more that you just can't control how much you eat once you start?\n\nPatient: Like I said a minute ago, it's definitely the control part. I don't really think it's about my feelings or ignoring them after work. It's just... once I start eating on those evenings, that autopilot feeling takes over. I can be standing right there literally telling myself to knock it off because I have to be up early for the crew, but I still end up going back to the kitchen for more anyway. I just lose the ability to control how much I'm having once I get going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating a lot?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel great about it because it messes with my sleep and I feel tired the next day at work, but I wouldn't call it guilt or shame. It's just frustrating that I can't seem to stop when I want to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any lack of interest in sex during the times when you're having these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any changes there. Um, my interest in sex is pretty normal. It hasn't really been affected by the eating stuff at all, as far as I can tell.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a lot happen?\n\nPatient: If I look back over the last few months, I'd say it averages out to about once a week. It fluctuates, though. Like, some weeks the whole pattern—the overeating and then purging—might happen twice, and then other weeks it won't happen at all. But yeah, on average, it's about a weekly thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or try to get rid of the food after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. A few times a month, after those nights where I eat a lot, I've gone into the bathroom and made myself throw up. I just kind of panic about undoing all the work I've put into looking put-together, you know? So I do it to try and get rid of the food.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself vomit after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying a minute ago, if I look back over the last few months, the whole pattern together—the overeating and then making myself throw up—averages out to about once a week. Some weeks it might happen twice, and other weeks not at all, but it generally averages out to a weekly thing.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself vomit after eating a lot?\n\nPatient: I think I just mentioned this, but yeah... looking back over the last few months, that whole pattern of overeating and then throwing up has averaged out to about once a week. Some weeks it happens twice, and other weeks it doesn't happen at all, but it generally comes out to a weekly thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you always make yourself vomit after one of these episodes of eating a lot?\n\nPatient: No, not always. Um... sometimes I overeat and I just leave it at that. I don't go to the bathroom to throw up every single time it happens.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It's just something that happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to resist the urge to continue eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's exactly what it feels like. Once I start on those evenings, it feels like I’m on autopilot. I'll be telling myself to stop because I have an early crew start the next morning, but I just... I still keep going back to the kitchen for more. I just can't seem to stop once I get going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating very quickly during these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm not really paying attention to the clock or anything, but I wouldn't say I'm eating unusually fast. It's more about the amount of food and just not being able to stop, rather than how quickly I'm eating it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the eating happens because you're not paying attention to your feelings after a long day, or is it more that you just can't control how much you eat once you start?\n\nPatient: Like I said a minute ago, it's definitely the control part. I don't really think it's about my feelings or ignoring them after work. It's just... once I start eating on those evenings, that autopilot feeling takes over. I can be standing right there literally telling myself to knock it off because I have to be up early for the crew, but I still end up going back to the kitchen for more anyway. I just lose the ability to control how much I'm having once I get going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating a lot?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel great about it because it messes with my sleep and I feel tired the next day at work, but I wouldn't call it guilt or shame. It's just frustrating that I can't seem to stop when I want to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any lack of interest in sex during the times when you're having these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any changes there. Um, my interest in sex is pretty normal. It hasn't really been affected by the eating stuff at all, as far as I can tell.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a lot happen?\n\nPatient: If I look back over the last few months, I'd say it averages out to about once a week. It fluctuates, though. Like, some weeks the whole pattern—the overeating and then purging—might happen twice, and then other weeks it won't happen at all. But yeah, on average, it's about a weekly thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or try to get rid of the food after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. A few times a month, after those nights where I eat a lot, I've gone into the bathroom and made myself throw up. I just kind of panic about undoing all the work I've put into looking put-together, you know? So I do it to try and get rid of the food.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself vomit after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying a minute ago, if I look back over the last few months, the whole pattern together—the overeating and then making myself throw up—averages out to about once a week. Some weeks it might happen twice, and other weeks not at all, but it generally averages out to a weekly thing.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself vomit after eating a lot?\n\nPatient: I think I just mentioned this, but yeah... looking back over the last few months, that whole pattern of overeating and then throwing up has averaged out to about once a week. Some weeks it happens twice, and other weeks it doesn't happen at all, but it generally comes out to a weekly thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you always make yourself vomit after one of these episodes of eating a lot?\n\nPatient: No, not always. Um... sometimes I overeat and I just leave it at that. I don't go to the bathroom to throw up every single time it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually make yourself vomit after these episodes of eating a lot?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if I'd say usually. Like I just mentioned a second ago, I definitely don't do it every time. I haven't really kept track of the exact ratio of when I do versus when I just overeat, you know? I just know that, overall, the throwing up part happens about once a week."
